#4905. Google says hackers use AI agents to find and exploit software flaws

Google says attackers are using AI agents to find and exploit software flaws automatically, reducing the time organizations have to apply patches.

Google Threat Intelligence Group (GTIG) said it had, for the first time, identified attackers using AI large language model (LLM) frameworks to automate software vulnerability discovery and exploitation without step-by-step human direction.

In a report published Monday, the researchers described a tool found on an exposed staging server that compared software versions, identified potential flaws, and generated and executed exploit code.

“In the past quarter, GTIG identified threat actors using LLM frameworks to fully automate vulnerability discovery and exploitation without a human in the loop for the first time,” the researchers said.

GTIG identified an exposed staging server hosting a multi-agent vulnerability research and weaponization framework, set up to conduct continuous differential analysis comparing code before and after patch announcements on popular web applications. The model would autonomously discover and exploit software vulnerabilities.

Google warned that these automated workflows give organizations less time to apply security patches before attackers develop ways to exploit vulnerable systems.

In its September report, GTIG said it had “not yet observed threat actors deploying fully autonomous pipelines against targets in the wild.”

Evolution of threat actors using LLMs for exploits. Source: Google

Attackers are leveraging AI agent frameworks to automate patch reverse engineering via differential analysis and weaponizing N-day vulnerabilities, publicly known security flaws.

The report warned that LLMs are enabling attackers to scale “exploitation pipelines against internet-facing servers without human intervention.”

Vulnerability disclosures more than doubled between January and August, according to Google. The number of zero-day vulnerabilities exploited averaged eight per month in 2025 and 11 per month from January through August 2026, reaching 22 in August.

Count of vulnerabilities exploited, by n-days and zero-days, January 2025 to August 2026. Source: Google

These automated workflows also accelerate attackers’ ability to move from initial access to gaining domain administrator access. The report cited an August 2026 PaperCut campaign in which attackers achieved that level of control within minutes, according to cybersecurity firm GreyNoise.

Related: PHP flaw shows AI ‘vulnerability apocalypse’ is on schedule: Immunefi CEO

Cloudflare also reported attempts to exploit the PHP flaws against its customers in October. GTIG said that the two PHP vulnerabilities involved in the exploitation attempts were discovered using LLM analysis, as technical information published before the Oct. 21 patch enabled researchers and attackers to study vulnerabilities and attempt exploits, according to the report.

AI company Anthropic also warned that AI-assisted attacks have evolved into multi-agent operations automating most of the cyberattack cycle from discovery to exploitation and data theft, in a September report.

Anthropic described threat actors running “multi-victim campaigns using agentic AI that would previously have required teams of operators.”

#4903. Toyota updates the 2027 RAV4 PHEV with an NACS port and up to 52 miles of EV range

The 2027 Toyota RAV4 is bringing a few major updates, including an added NACS charge port on all plug-in hybrid (PHEV) models.

The RAV4 was Toyota’s best-selling nameplate in the US last year and consistently ranks among America’s most popular vehicles alongside the Ford F-Series and Chevrolet Silverado.

For the 2027 model year, Toyota added several notable upgrades. The 2027 Toyota RAV4 will be available with hybrid (HEV) and plug-in hybrid (PHEV) powertrains, with available front- or all-wheel drive (AWD).

The biggest change is that all 2027 RAV4 PHEV models now have a built-in NACS charge port with Level 3 DC fast-charging capabilities.

The NACS port is on the driver’s side and lets drivers use Tesla Superchargers without a separate adapter. All PHEV grades also include an 11 kW onboard AC charger.

Powered by Toyota’s sixth-generation PHEV system, the plug-in RAV4 has standard AWD with a combined output of 321 horsepower. The setup includes a 2.5-liter four-cylinder engine, two electric motors, and a high-capacity traction battery.

The 2027 Toyota RAV4 PHEV offers up to 52 miles of pure electric range on the base SE trim. It will be available in four trims: SE, XSE, Woodland, and GR Sport.

Toyota updated the suspension system in the Woodland grade for the 2027 model year with new springs, shocks, and jounce bumpers, giving it 9.1″ of ground clearance.

The Woodland trim adds a rugged look with standard All-Terrain tires, Roof Rails with Cross Bars, and a Tow Hitch Receiver. It also features new all-weather floor and cargo mats.

Lastly, the RAV4 GR Sport trim is for those who want a more performance-oriented, sporty style, with GT-tuned suspension and steering, sports seating, and a standard PHEV powertrain with AWD.

Inside, the 2027 RAV4 includes a standard 12.3″ digital cluster and a 10.5″ touchscreen with the Toyota Audio Multimedia system, plus wireless Apple CarPlay and Android Auto.

A 12.9″ touchscreen with a JBL Premium Audio system with 9 speakers is standard on the Limited trim and available on the XSE grade.

All 2027 Toyota RAV4s come with standard Toyota Safety Sense 4.0, which includes safety and convenience features such as Lane Departure Alert with Steering Assist, Full-Speed Range Dynamic Radar Cruise Control, Proactive Driving Assist, and a Pre-Collision System with Pedestrian Detection.

They don't make it easy to find out just what that level 3 charging rate is or how big the battery is. If it's as slow as an old Bolt, could take as long as 20 minutes to charge. If the DC charge hardware is more modern, could be 5-10. Would still be a weird choice, but if you need to stop for a drink of a bathroom anyway, why not?

2027 Toyota RAV4 HEV models are expected to begin arriving at dealerships later this year, while the PHEV models are expected to follow in early 2027.

The 2027 Toyota RAV4 HEV starts at $32,300 (LE FWD), and the PHEV version starts at $42,100, not including the $1,595 destination fee.

#4902. Morning Minute: The CFTC Officially Greenlights Perps in the US, Starting with Backpack

Morning Minute: The CFTC Officially Greenlights Perps in the US, Starting with Backpack

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

🏛️ Backpack and tZERO Win the First Onchain Derivatives Approvals

For years, perps were the product American traders couldn’t legally touch. On Monday, that officially changed.

Chairman Michael Selig approved applications from Backpack and tZERO to run regulated derivatives exchanges and clearinghouses that execute and settle on blockchain rails. Selig called it “an important milestone in the evolution of American financial markets.”

Backpack got both pieces at once, a Designated Contract Market designation to run the exchange and a Derivatives Clearing Organization registration to clear its own trades. It plans to offer Americans round-the-clock leveraged crypto perps on a single global order book, accepting both fiat and crypto as collateral with cross-margin across positions. tZERO received its exchange approval a week earlier, after already holding clearing registration, and is building it into a derivatives vertical inside its infrastructure business.

Backpack US president Mark Wetjen, himself a former CFTC commissioner, framed it as “moving price discovery from offshore unregulated venues to the United States.” CEO Armani Ferrante said the firm enters markets “fully regulated from day one.” Backpack already runs licensed platforms in Europe, Asia and the UAE.

The CFTC has certainly been busy this month. They cleared single-stock perpetual futures on November 3. It issued guidance on November 5 telling federally regulated exchanges they generally cannot restrict access by state. Now it has approved blockchain-native exchanges and clearinghouses.

Selig is building an onshore home for every product the offshore venues were built to serve, and he is doing it through staff action rather than legislation, with one sitting commissioner and nomination hearings for two more this week.

Hyperliquid is the obvious candidate and also the one that has publicly said it doesn’t want this. Jeff Yan spent his Bloomberg interview this month arguing Hyperliquid is infrastructure rather than an exchange, and that regulators should oversee the firms deploying markets on top rather than the chain underneath. His US route is HIP-3 partners taking on the compliance burden, with ICE investing in Trade.xyz and Kraken’s parent reportedly in talks. Backpack getting a DCM and a DCO makes that argument harder, because it demonstrates a crypto-native venue can simply register.

Then there’s Lighter. It has run Robinhood Wallet’s perps since July on a dedicated instance with a 50/50 revenue split, which is the partner model rather than the registration model. The registered model opens up with a CFTC license, and many think Lighter could very well be next.

One thing’s certain—the CFTC is serious about bringing perps onshore. From Selig himself, “I look forward to building on that milestone.” And we will find out soon enough who’s next…

#4900. Bitcoin realized loss metric beats June high as open interest eyes 2-year low

Bitcoin short-term holders’ onchain spending notched the largest proportion of realized losses since February after BTC/USD dropped to $62,000.

Bitcoin investors are showing increasing signs of panic as newer buyers’ onchain losses hit their highest since February.

Data from onchain analytics platform CryptoQuant reveals new nine-month lows in the spent output profit ratio (SOPR) of Bitcoin’s short-term holders (STH).

SOPR measures the ratio of BTC moving onchain at a higher or lower price relative to the last time it was used in a UTXO. The metric can be broken down by investor cohort, only accounting for coins owned by older or newer hodlers. The latter cohort traditionally consists of speculative entities who are more sensitive to BTC price volatility. An STH entity is a wallet holding a given unit of BTC for up to 155 days without selling.

On Nov. 16, STH-SOPR recorded a reading of 0.955, its lowest since Feb. 7. Readings below 1 reflect STH coins moving onchain on aggregate at a lower price compared to their previous transaction.

Noticeable deviations below 1 on STH-SOPR imply increased loss-making selling activity by the STH cohort. While BTC/USD briefly dipped to $62,000 this week, the latest reading is notably lower than in late June, when investors reacted to price hitting 20-month lows of $58,534 and STH-SOPR reached 0.985.

In its latest analysis released on Monday, Bitfinex Alpha, the research arm of crypto exchange Bitfinex, noted that in both June and July, swing lows on STH-SOPR corresponded to the “days of heaviest loss-taking” among speculators.

“Net realised losses across all holders reached about 76,500 BTC on 15 November, the largest since 1 July, a significant increase from about 18,700 BTC on 10 November,” it reported.

Bitcoin STH profitability comparison. Source: Bitfinex

Bitfinex added that unrealized losses reveal the pain levels of the broader Bitcoin investor base, with the share of the BTC supply held at loss reaching 48.5% on Nov. 14 — the same as on July 1, when BTC/USD set a new multiyear floor of $57,800.

“Holders are therefore under as much pressure as they were at that low, with price about 11 percent higher,” it wrote.

Speculative Bitcoin trading activity overall has witnessed a marked drop in recent weeks.

Related: BTC price tags 18-week low into Nvidia earnings: Three things to know in Bitcoin this week

Open interest, the total number of active derivative positions, dropped to $20.498 billion on Tuesday, a level not seen since late 2024, as measured by CryptoQuant.

In commentary on Monday, CryptoQuant contributor Baykuş flagged a conspicuous drop in OI on Binance as BTC/USD fell from $74,000 to $64,000.

“Open interest on coin-margined BTC-USD contracts fell from $1.55B to $1.1B, and since these contracts are USD-denominated, this reflects real position closures,” they wrote in a blog post.

#4899. BlackRock’s Bitcoin ETF snaps $2.35B outflow streak amid extreme fear

BlackRock’s Bitcoin ETF ended 11 sessions of withdrawals totaling $2.35 billion, but CryptoQuant says one day of inflows is too early to signal a recovery.

BlackRock’s spot Bitcoin exchange-traded fund (ETF) returned to inflows Monday, ending an 11-session outflow streak totaling roughly $2.35 billion.

The iShares Bitcoin Trust (IBIT) attracted $76.5 million on Nov. 16, helping US spot Bitcoin ETFs record $7.9 million in net inflows despite withdrawals from several other funds, according to Farside Investors data.

The inflows came as Bitcoin (BTC) traded near multimonth lows of $64,000, while the Crypto Fear and Greed Index, which tracks cryptocurrency market sentiment, fell to 10 out of 100 on Monday, its lowest reading since June 2026.

Nick Pitto, head of marketing at blockchain analytics company CryptoQuant, cautioned against interpreting the inflows as a broader market recovery.

“One day of inflows doesn’t change the picture too much,” Pitto said. He noted that Monday’s inflows recovered only about 3% of IBIT’s withdrawals over the previous 11 sessions, while US Bitcoin ETFs had shed more than 31,000 BTC over the past month.

“It’s too early to call this dip buying,” he said, adding: “We’d need to see several days of inflows in a row, and from more funds than just BlackRock’s.”

Salman Parcy, managing partner at crypto investment company Cequire Capital, offered a more optimistic interpretation, suggesting that some institutional investors may see Bitcoin’s recent decline as a buying opportunity.

“While it is still early to call this a reversal, the return of capital to IBIT does show that some institutional investors are beginning to see an opportunity in current price levels,” Parcy said.

Related: Bitcoin price fails to hold $65K as US stocks drop further on AI nerves

Monday also marked the deadline for most institutions to file their third-quarter Form 13F disclosures, revealing Bitcoin ETF holdings as of Sept. 30. Several institutions reported increased exposure during the quarter, but Pitto cautioned against linking those holdings to Monday’s inflows.

“ETF buying peaked in August and September. But the filings only show what they held at the end of September, not what they’ve done since. So they may not explain Monday’s inflow,” he said.

#4897. New Jersey is putting 10 MW of community solar on warehouse roofs

Warehouse roofs are prime real estate for solar, and Faropoint is putting more of that underused space to work in New Jersey. The industrial property owner has started construction on the first of 16 community solar projects totaling more than 10 megawatts (MW).

First up is a 1.6 MW rooftop array at 19-05 Nevins Road in Fair Lawn. Another 15 projects, totaling about 8.5 MW, have been accepted into New Jersey’s Community Solar Energy Program, and nine are already cleared to start construction.

That’s more solar going onto buildings that are already there, with participating households set to get savings on their electricity bills.

Large warehouse roofs offer something solar developers need: lots of flat space. Putting panels up there gives the same building the job of generating electricity without taking up additional land.

Faropoint specializes in industrial properties used for local deliveries and distribution. “Utilizing previously unused portions of our buildings allows us to support the communities where we invest,” said Brian Nave, Faropoint’s vice president of asset management and renewable energy.

Faropoint and its independent solar adviser, SolarKal, expect the 16 projects to generate around 12.7 million kilowatt-hours (kWh) of electricity annually once they’re operating. They estimate the portfolio will avoid about 8,500 metric tons of carbon dioxide emissions a year.

Faropoint says at least half of each project’s capacity will be reserved for low- and moderate-income households. New Jersey’s program requires a minimum of 51%.

Excellent. There are so many warehouses around here, it always baffled me that they weren't all covered with solar panels. Next we gotta get to work on all the parking lots.

Faropoint and SolarKal have not yet announced completion dates, subscription prices, or enrollment details for their New Jersey warehouse roof community solar portfolio.

#4896. Standard Life Chair Wants Tokenized Gilts to Open UK Government Bonds to Retail Savers

Standard Life Chair Wants Tokenized Gilts to Open UK Government Bonds to Retail Savers

Tokenization could help ordinary British savers buy government bonds more easily, and should not become a tool only for big financial institutions, according to Sir Nicholas Lyons, chairman of Standard Life.

"Why should it be so unnecessarily complicated for retail investors to buy gilts that fund the nation's needs?" Lyons said at Solana's Breakpoint conference in London.

Lyons, a former Lord Mayor of the City of London whose company has about £480 billion ($633 billion) in assets under administration, said Britain should use tokenization to bring more households into investing. About £850 billion ($1.1 trillion) of household wealth remains in cash, often losing value to inflation, he said.

Tokenization could cut administrative costs, make transfers simpler and let savers invest in small amounts in products that would otherwise be uneconomic, Lyons said. More retail ownership of gilts would make the UK less reliant on foreign buyers and could help erode the risk premium the country has paid on its debt since the September 2022 mini-budget, he added.

The remarks come as the UK prepares to issue its first digitally native government bond, known as DIGIT. Earlier this month, the Treasury confirmed roles for six banks supporting the pilot, with issuance expected in the first quarter of 2027, Lyons said.

"Government bonds sit at the heart of our financial system," he said, noting that they underpin liquidity, provide collateral and set reference rates for other assets. Showing that tokenized gilts can be issued, settled and used as collateral efficiently would lay "an important foundation for much broader digital capital markets," he said.

Lyons credited the Financial Conduct Authority, the Bank of England and the Treasury for progress so far, including the Digital Securities Sandbox and work to let tokenized securities be used as collateral.

The Bank of England has also turned its attention to stablecoins: an executive has said it is treating them as a new form of money, it eased its planned stablecoin rules in June, and it has since been handed a legal duty to foster stablecoin innovation. Lyons cited a July report by Chris Woolard, the government's wholesale digital markets champion, which estimated that faster adoption of digital markets could add as much as £33 billion ($43.5 billion) a year to UK economic output by 2035.

But the competition is moving fast, he warned, pointing to the SEC's innovation exemption for tokenized stock trading in the U.S., and initiatives in Europe, Singapore, Hong Kong, Switzerland and the Middle East.

Lyons set out four priorities: making the digital gilt pilot a success, accelerating tokenized collateral and repo markets, promoting interoperability to avoid "a collection of competing digital platforms that recreate the fragmentation of the existing system," and ensuring retail investors share in the benefits.

He also acknowledged the risks, citing concerns raised by the Financial Times' Gillian Tett that AI-accelerated cyber threats and faster-moving money could fuel a run on deposits. "Tokenization does not automatically make an investment safer, more liquid, or more stable, or suitable for retail investors," he said. "Fractional ownership of a risky asset remains ownership of a risky asset."

"We're not pursuing tokenization for the sake of technological leadership," Lyons said. "We're pursuing better capital markets, more productive investment, greater economic prosperity, and wider participation in the creation of wealth and jobs."
